 A.2 Development of COM-MON 
 1. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟣𝟥 \mathsf{Thm13} : COM-MONOID ( U { M } , ⋅ ( M × M ) → M , 𝖾 M ) {\textsf{COM-MONOID}(U_{\{M\}},\cdot_{{({M}\times{M})}\rightarrow{M}},\mathsf{e}_{M})} 
 x (models of COM-MON define commutative monoids).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be COM-MON . We must show
 ( ⋆ ) T ⊨ COM-MONOID ( U { M } , ⋅ ( M × M ) → M , 𝖾 M ) . (\star){\ }T\vDash\textsf{COM-MONOID}(U_{\{M\}},\cdot_{{({M}\times{M})}\rightarrow{M}},\mathsf{e}_{M}). 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ 𝖬𝖮𝖭𝖮𝖨𝖣 ( U { M } , ⋅ ( M × M ) → M , 𝖾 M ) \displaystyle\vDash{\mathsf{MONOID}(U_{\{M\}},\cdot_{{({M}\times{M})}\rightarrow{M}},\mathsf{e}_{M})} 
 (1) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∀ x , y : U { M } . x ⋅ y = y ⋅ x \displaystyle\vDash{\forall\,x,y:U_{\{M\}}\mathrel{.}x\cdot y=y\cdot x} 
 (2) 
 (1) follows from MON ≤ T \textsf{MON}\leq T and the fact that
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟣 \mathsf{Thm1} is a theorem of MON ; and
(2) follows from Axiom 3 of T T and part 5 of Lemma  B.1 .
Therefore, ( ⋆ ) (\star)  follows from (1), (2), and the notational definition of
 COM-MONOID given in Table  3 .
 □ \Box 
 2. 
 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟧 \mathsf{Def5} : ≤ M → M → o = λ x , y : M . ∃ z : M . x ⋅ z = y {{\mathsf{\leq}_{{M}\rightarrow{{M}\rightarrow{o}}}}={\lambda\,x,y:M\mathrel{.}\exists\,z:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{z}}={y}}} (weak order).
 Proof that RHS is defined. 
Similar to the proof that the RHS of Def1 is defined.
 □ \Box 
 3. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟣𝟦 \mathsf{Thm14} : ∀ x : M . x ≤ x {\forall\,x:M\mathrel{.}{x}\mathrel{\leq}{x}} (reflexivity).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be COM-MON extended by Def5 .
We must show
 ( ⋆ ) ​ T ⊨ ∀ x : M . x ≤ x . (\star){\ }T\vDash{\forall\,x:M\mathrel{.}{x}\mathrel{\leq}{x}}. 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( x : M ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash{({x}:{M}){\downarrow}} 
 (1) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( x ≤ x ) ≃ ( ∃ z : M . x ⋅ z = x ) \displaystyle\vDash{{({x}\mathrel{\leq}{x})}\simeq{(\exists\,z:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{z}}={x})}} 
 (2) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ x ⋅ 𝖾 = x \displaystyle\vDash{{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{\mathsf{e}}}={x}} 
 (3) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∃ z : M . x ⋅ z = x \displaystyle\vDash{\exists\,z:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{z}}={x}} 
 (4) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ x ≤ x \displaystyle\vDash{{x}\mathrel{\leq}{x}} 
 (5) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∀ x : M . x ≤ x \displaystyle\vDash{\forall\,x:M\mathrel{.}{x}\mathrel{\leq}{x}} 
 (6) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;
(2) follows from Def5 and Extensionality  [ 20 , Axiom A3] using
the Substitution Rule  [ 20 , Theorem A.31] and
Beta-Reduction  [ 20 , Theorem 7.1 and Axiom A4] ;
(3) follows from (1) and Axiom 2 of T T by Universal
Instantiation  [ 20 , Theorem A.14] ;
(4) follows from (3) by Existential Generalization [ 20 , Theorem A.51] ;
(5) follows from (2) and (4) by Rule R2 ′ \mbox{R2}^{\prime} [ 20 , Lemma A.2] ; and
(6) follows from (5) by Universal Generalization [ 20 , Theorem A.30] 
using the fact that x x is not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences.
Therefore, ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds.
 □ \Box 
 4. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟣𝟧 \mathsf{Thm15} : ∀ x , y , z : M . ( x ≤ y ∧ y ≤ z ) ⇒ x ≤ z {\forall\,x,y,z:M\mathrel{.}{({{x}\mathrel{\leq}{y}}\wedge{{y}\mathrel{\leq}{z}})}\Rightarrow{{x}\mathrel{\leq}{z}}} (transitivity).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let A o \textbf{A}_{o} be ( x ≤ y ∧ y ≤ z ) {({{x}\mathrel{\leq}{y}}\wedge{{y}\mathrel{\leq}{z}})} , B o \textbf{B}_{o} be x ⋅ u = y {{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{u}}={y}} , and C o \textbf{C}_{o} be y ⋅ v = z {{{y}\mathrel{\cdot}{v}}={z}} (where these variables all have type M M ). Also let T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be COM-MON extended by Def5 . We must
show
 ( ⋆ ) ​ T ⊨ ∀ x , y , z : M . A o ⇒ x ≤ z . (\star){\ }T\vDash{\forall\,x,y,z:M\mathrel{.}{\textbf{A}_{o}}\Rightarrow{{x}\mathrel{\leq}{z}}}. 
 Γ ∪ { B o , C o } \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\{\textbf{B}_{o},\textbf{C}_{o}\} 
 ⊨ ( x : M ) ↓ ∧ ( y : M ) ↓ ∧ ( z : M ) ↓ ∧ ( u : M ) ↓ ∧ \displaystyle\vDash{({x}:{M}){\downarrow}}\land{({y}:{M}){\downarrow}}\land{({z}:{M}){\downarrow}}\land{({u}:{M}){\downarrow}}\land{} 
 ( v : M ) ↓ \displaystyle\phantom{{}\vDash{}}\hskip 8.61108pt{({v}:{M}){\downarrow}} 
 (1) 
 Γ ∪ { B o , C o } \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\{\textbf{B}_{o},\textbf{C}_{o}\} 
 ⊨ ( x ⋅ u ) ⋅ v = z \displaystyle\vDash{{{({x}\mathrel{\cdot}{u})}\mathrel{\cdot}{v}}={z}} 
 (2) 
 Γ ∪ { B o , C o } \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\{\textbf{B}_{o},\textbf{C}_{o}\} 
 ⊨ ( x ⋅ u ) ⋅ v = x ⋅ ( u ⋅ v ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{({x}\mathrel{\cdot}{u})}\mathrel{\cdot}{v}}={{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{({u}\mathrel{\cdot}{v})}}} 
 (3) 
 Γ ∪ { B o , C o } \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\{\textbf{B}_{o},\textbf{C}_{o}\} 
 ⊨ x ⋅ ( u ⋅ v ) = z \displaystyle\vDash{{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{({u}\mathrel{\cdot}{v})}}={z}} 
 (4) 
 Γ ∪ { B o , C o } \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\{\textbf{B}_{o},\textbf{C}_{o}\} 
 ⊨ ∃ w : M . x ⋅ w = z \displaystyle\vDash{\exists\,w:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{w}}={z}} 
 (5) 
 Γ ∪ { B o } \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\{\textbf{B}_{o}\} 
 ⊨ ( y ⋅ v = z ) ⇒ ( ∃ w : M . x ⋅ w = z ) \displaystyle\vDash{{({{y}\mathrel{\cdot}{v}}={z})}\Rightarrow{(\exists\,w:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{w}}={z})}} 
 (6) 
 Γ ∪ { B o } \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\{\textbf{B}_{o}\} 
 ⊨ ( ∃ v : M . y ⋅ v = z ) ⇒ ( ∃ w : M . x ⋅ w = z ) \displaystyle\vDash{{(\exists\,v:M\mathrel{.}{{y}\mathrel{\cdot}{v}}={z})}\Rightarrow{(\exists\,w:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{w}}={z})}} 
 (7) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( x ⋅ u = y ) ⇒ \displaystyle\vDash{{({{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{u}}={y})}\Rightarrow{}} 
 ( ( ∃ v : M . y ⋅ v = z ) ⇒ ( ∃ w : M . x ⋅ w = z ) ) \displaystyle\phantom{{}\vDash{}}\hskip 8.61108pt{({(\exists\,v:M\mathrel{.}{{y}\mathrel{\cdot}{v}}={z})}\Rightarrow{(\exists\,w:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{w}}={z})})} 
 (8) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( ∃ u : M . x ⋅ u = y ) ⇒ \displaystyle\vDash{{(\exists\,u:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{u}}={y})}\Rightarrow{}} 
 ( ( ∃ v : M . y ⋅ v = z ) ⇒ ( ∃ w : M . x ⋅ w = z ) ) \displaystyle\phantom{{}\vDash{}}\hskip 8.61108pt{({(\exists\,v:M\mathrel{.}{{y}\mathrel{\cdot}{v}}={z})}\Rightarrow{(\exists\,w:M\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{w}}={z})})} 
 (9) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ x ≤ y ⇒ ( y ≤ z ⇒ x ≤ z ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{x}\mathrel{\leq}{y}}\Rightarrow{({{y}\mathrel{\leq}{z}}\Rightarrow{{x}\mathrel{\leq}{z}})}} 
 (10) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ A o ⇒ x ≤ z \displaystyle\vDash{{\textbf{A}_{o}}\Rightarrow{{x}\mathrel{\leq}{z}}} 
 (11) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∀ x , y , z : M . A o ⇒ x ≤ z \displaystyle\vDash{\forall\,x,y,z:M\mathrel{.}{\textbf{A}_{o}}\Rightarrow{{x}\mathrel{\leq}{z}}} 
 (12) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;
(2) follows from B o \textbf{B}_{o} and C o \textbf{C}_{o} by the
Equality Rules [ 20 , Lemma A.13] ;
(3) follows from Axiom 1 of T T ;
(4) follows from (2) and (3) by the Equality Rules [ 20 , Lemma A.13] ;
(5) follows from (1), (4), and 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟤 \mathsf{Thm2} by Existential Generalization
 [ 20 , Theorem A.51] ;
(6) and (8) follow from (5) and (7), respectively, by the Deduction Theorem
 [ 20 , Theorem A.50] ;
(7) and (9) follow from (6) and (8), respectively, by
Existential Instantiation (Theorem B.3);
(10) follows from (1), (9), and 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟧 \mathsf{Def5} by Beta-Reduction
 [ 20 , Theorem 7.1 and Axiom A4] and
Alpha-Conversion [ 20 , Theorem 7.6 and Theorem A.18] ;
(11) follows from (10) by the Tautology Rule [ 20 , Corollary A.46] ; and
(12) follows from (11) by Universal Generalization [ 20 , Theorem A.30] 
using the fact that x x , y y , and z z are not free in Γ \Gamma 
since Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences.
Therefore, ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds.
 □ \Box 
 A.3 Development of FUN-COMP 
 1. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟣𝟨 \mathsf{Thm16} :
 ∀ f : A → B , g : B → C , h : C → D . f ∘ ( g ∘ h ) = ( f ∘ g ) ∘ h {\forall\,f:{A}\rightarrow{B},\,g:{B}\rightarrow{C},\,h:{C}\rightarrow{D}\mathrel{.}{f\circ{({g}\circ{h})}}={({f}\circ{g})\circ{h}}} 
 x ( ∘ \circ is associative).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let A o \textbf{A}_{o} be the theorem and T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be
 FUN-COMP . We must show ( ⋆ ) ​ T ⊨ A o (\star){\ }T\vDash\textbf{A}_{o} .
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( f : A → B ) ↓ ∧ ( g : B → C ) ↓ ∧ ( h : C → D ) ↓ ∧ ( x : A ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash{({f}:{{A}\rightarrow{B}}){\downarrow}}\land{({g}:{{B}\rightarrow{C}}){\downarrow}}\land{({h}:{{C}\rightarrow{D}}){\downarrow}}\land{({x}:{A}){\downarrow}} 
 (1) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( ( f ∘ g ) ∘ h ) ​ x ≃ h ⁡ ( g ⁡ ( f ​ x ) ) \displaystyle\vDash{{((f\circ g)\circ h)\,x}\simeq{h\,(g\,(f\,x))}} 
 (2) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( f ∘ ( g ∘ h ) ) ​ x ≃ h ⁡ ( g ⁡ ( f ​ x ) ) \displaystyle\vDash{{(f\circ(g\circ h))\,x}\simeq{h\,(g\,(f\,x))}} 
 (3) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( ( f ∘ g ) ∘ h ) ​ x ≃ ( f ∘ ( g ∘ h ) ) ​ x \displaystyle\vDash{{((f\circ g)\circ h)\,x}\simeq{(f\circ(g\circ h))\,x}} 
 (4) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∀ x : A . ( f ∘ ( g ∘ h ) ) ​ x ≃ ( ( f ∘ g ) ∘ h ) ​ x \displaystyle\vDash{\forall\,x:A\mathrel{.}(f\circ(g\circ h))\,x\simeq{((f\circ g)\circ h)\,x}} 
 (5) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ f ∘ ( g ∘ h ) = ( f ∘ g ) ∘ h \displaystyle\vDash f\circ(g\circ h)=(f\circ g)\circ h 
 (6) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ A o \displaystyle\vDash\textbf{A}_{o} 
 (7) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;
(2) and (3) both follow from (1), the definition of ∘ \circ in
Table  2 , function abstractions are always defined by
 [ 20 , Axiom A5.11] , ordered pairs of defined components are always
defined by [ 20 , Axiom A7.1] , Beta-Reduction
 [ 20 , Theorem 7.1 and Axiom A4] , and
Quasi-Equality Substitution [ 20 , Lemma A.2] ;
(4) follows from (2) and (3) by the Quasi-Equality Rules [ 20 , Lemma A.4] ;
(5) follows from (4) by Universal Generalization [ 20 , Theorem A.30] 
using the fact that x x is not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences;
(6) follows from (5) by Extensionality [ 20 , Axiom A3] ; and
(7) follows from (6) by Universal Generalization using the fact that
 f f , g g , and h h are not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences.
Therefore, ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds.
 □ \Box 
 2. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟣𝟩 \mathsf{Thm17} :
 ∀ f : A → B . 𝗂𝖽 A → A ∘ f = f ∘ 𝗂𝖽 B → B = f {\forall\,f:{A}\rightarrow{B}\mathrel{.}{\mathsf{id}_{{A}\rightarrow{A}}\circ{f}}\mathrel{=}{f\circ{\mathsf{id}_{{B}\rightarrow{B}}}}\mathrel{=}{f}} 
 x (identity functions are left and right identity elements).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let A o \textbf{A}_{o} be the theorem and T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be
 FUN-COMP . We must show ( ⋆ ) ​ T ⊨ A o (\star){\ }T\vDash\textbf{A}_{o} .
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( f : A → B ) ↓ ∧ ( x : A ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash{{({f}:{{A}\rightarrow{B}}){\downarrow}}\wedge{({x}:{A}){\downarrow}}} 
 (1) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( 𝗂𝖽 A → A ∘ f ) ​ x ≃ f ​ x \displaystyle\vDash{{({\mathsf{id}_{{A}\rightarrow{A}}}\mathrel{\circ}{f})\,x}\simeq{f\,x}} 
 (2) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( f ∘ 𝗂𝖽 B → B ) ​ x ≃ f ​ x \displaystyle\vDash{{({f}\mathrel{\circ}{\mathsf{id}_{{B}\rightarrow{B}}})\,x}\simeq{f\,x}} 
 (3) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∀ x : A . ( 𝗂𝖽 A → A ∘ f ) ​ x ≃ f ​ x \displaystyle\vDash{\forall\,x:A\mathrel{.}{({\mathsf{id}_{{A}\rightarrow{A}}}\mathrel{\circ}{f})\,x}\simeq{f\,x}} 
 (4) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∀ x : A . ( f ∘ 𝗂𝖽 B → B ) ​ x ≃ f ​ x \displaystyle\vDash{\forall\,x:A\mathrel{.}{({f}\mathrel{\circ}{\mathsf{id}_{{B}\rightarrow{B}}})\,x}\simeq{f\,x}} 
 (5) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ 𝗂𝖽 A → A ∘ f = f \displaystyle\vDash{{{\mathsf{id}_{{A}\rightarrow{A}}}\mathrel{\circ}{f}}={f}} 
 (6)

 ⊨ f ∘ 𝗂𝖽 B → B = f \displaystyle\vDash{{{f}\mathrel{\circ}{\mathsf{id}_{{B}\rightarrow{B}}}}={f}} 
 (7)

 ⊨ 𝗂𝖽 A → A ∘ f = f ∘ 𝗂𝖽 B → B = f \displaystyle\vDash{{\mathsf{id}_{{A}\rightarrow{A}}\circ{f}}\mathrel{=}{f\circ{\mathsf{id}_{{B}\rightarrow{B}}}}\mathrel{=}{f}} 
 (8)

 ⊨ A o \displaystyle\vDash\textbf{A}_{o}

 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;

in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences;
(6) and (7) follow from (4) and (5), respectively, by Extensionality

(8) follows from (6) and (7) by the Equality Rules  [ 20 , Lemma A.13] ;

 Def6 (Def1-via-MON-to-ONE-BT) :

 x (models of MON-ACT define monoid actions).

 4.

and Def8 . We must show
 ( ⋆ ) ​ T ⊨ ∀ s : S . 𝗌𝗎𝖻𝗆𝗈𝗇𝗈𝗂𝖽 ⁡ ( 𝗌𝗍𝖺𝖻𝗂𝗅𝗂𝗓𝖾𝗋 ​ s ) . (\star){\ }T\vDash{\forall\,s:S\mathrel{.}\mathsf{submonoid}\,(\mathsf{stabilizer}\,s)}.

 ⊨ ∀ s : S . 𝗌𝗎𝖻𝗆𝗈𝗇𝗈𝗂𝖽 ⁡ ( 𝗌𝗍𝖺𝖻𝗂𝗅𝗂𝗓𝖾𝗋 ​ s ) \displaystyle\vDash\forall\,s:S\mathrel{.}\mathsf{submonoid}\,(\mathsf{stabilizer}\,s) 
 (10) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axioms A5.1] ;
(2) follows from constants always being defined by  [ 20 , Axioms A5.2] ;
(3) follows from Def8 by the Equality Rules  [ 20 , Lemma A.13] and
Beta-Reduction [ 20 , Axiom A4] applied to (1) and the RHS of the result;
(4) follows from (3) and Axiom 4 of T T ;
(5) follows immediately from (4);
(6) follows from Thm2 , (3), and Axiom 3 of T T ;
(7) follows from (3) and [ 20 , Axiom A5.4] ;
(8) follows from Def1 by the Equality Rules and Beta-Reduction
applied to (7) and the RHS of the result;
(9) follows from (4), (5), (6), and (8) by the Tautology
Rule  [ 20 , Corollary A.46] ;
(10) follows from (9) by Universal Generalization [ 20 , Theorem A.30] 
using the fact that s s is free in Γ \Gamma because Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences.
Therefore, ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds.
 □ \Box

 A.8 Development of MON-over-COF 
 1. 
 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟫 \mathsf{Def9} : 𝗉𝗋𝗈𝖽 R → R → ( R → M ) → M = {{\mathsf{prod}_{{R}\rightarrow{{R}\rightarrow{{({R}\rightarrow{M})}\rightarrow{M}}}}}={}} 
 I ​ f : Z { R } → Z { R } → ( Z { R } → M ) → M . {\mathrm{I}\,f:{Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{{Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{{({Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{M})}\rightarrow{M}}}\mathrel{.}} 
    ∀ m , n : Z { R } , g : Z { R } → M . f ​ m ​ n ​ g ≃ {\forall\,m,n:Z_{\{R\}},\,g:{Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{M}\mathrel{.}{f\,m\,n\,g}\simeq{}} 
    ( m > n ↦ 𝖾 ∣ ( f ​ m ​ ( n - 1 ) ​ g ) ⋅ ( g ​ n ) ) {({m}\mathrel{>}{n}\mapsto\mathsf{e}\mid{(f\,m\,({n}\mathrel{-}{1})\,g)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(g\,n)})} (iterated product).
 Proof that RHS is defined. 
Let
 A o = ∀ m , n : Z { R } , g : Z { R } → M . f ​ m ​ n ​ g ≃ \displaystyle\textbf{A}_{o}={\forall\,m,n:Z_{\{R\}},\,g:{Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{M}\mathrel{.}{f\,m\,n\,g}\simeq{}} 
 ( m > n ↦ 𝖾 ∣ ( f ​ m ​ ( n - 1 ) ​ g ) ⋅ ( g ​ n ) ) . \displaystyle\hskip 25.83325pt{({m}\mathrel{>}{n}\mapsto\mathsf{e}\mid{(f\,m\,({n}\mathrel{-}{1})\,g)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(g\,n)})}. 
 Suppose that two functions f 1 f_{1} and f 2 f_{2} satisfy
 A o \textbf{A}_{o} . It is easy to see that f 1 f_{1} and f 2 f_{2} must be the
same function based on the recursive structure of f f in
 A o \textbf{A}_{o} . Thus, A o \textbf{A}_{o} specifies a unique function,
and so the RHS of 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟫 \mathsf{Def9} is defined by [ 20 , Axiom
A6.1] .
 □ \Box 
 2.

 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;

 □ \Box 
 3. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟥𝟢 \mathsf{Thm30} :
 ∀ m , k , n : Z { R } , g : Z { R } → M . {\forall\,m,k,n:Z_{\{R\}},\,g:{Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{M}\mathrel{.}}

 x (extended iterated product).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let 𝐀 o \mathbf{A}_{o} be the theorem and T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be

 Let Δ \Delta be the set
 { m ∈ Z { R } , k ∈ Z { R } , n ∈ Z { R } , m < k < n } . \{{{m}\in{Z_{\{R\}}}},{{k}\in{Z_{\{R\}}}},{{n}\in{Z_{\{R\}}}},{{m}\mathrel{<}{k}\mathrel{<}{n}}\}. 
 We will prove
 ( b ) ​ Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = k + 1 n g ​ i ) ≃ ( ∏ i = m n g ​ i ) (\mbox{b}){\ }\Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={k+1}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}} 
 from all n > k n>k by induction on the n n .

 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( m : R ) ↓ ∧ ( k : R ) ↓ ∧ ( n : R ) ↓ ∧ ( g : R → M ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash{({m}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({k}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({n}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({g}:{{R}\rightarrow{M}}){\downarrow}} 
 (1) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = k + 1 n g ​ i ) ≃ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n \displaystyle\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={k+1}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{g\,n}}} 
 (2) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n ≃ ( ∏ i = m n g ​ i ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{g\,n}}\simeq{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}} 
 (3) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;
(2) follows from n = k + 1 n=k+1 and 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟤𝟫 \mathsf{Thm29} ; and
(3) follows from n = k + 1 n=k+1 , (1), and 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟫 \mathsf{Def9} .
Thus (b) holds by the Quasi-Equality Rules [ 20 , Lemma A.4] 
when n = k + 1 n=k+1 .
 Induction step: n > k + 1 n>k+1 and assume 
 Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = k + 1 n − 1 g ​ i ) ≃ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 g ​ i ) . \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={k+1}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}}.

 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( m : R ) ↓ ∧ ( k : R ) ↓ ∧ ( n : R ) ↓ ∧ ( g : R → M ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash{({m}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({k}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({n}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({g}:{{R}\rightarrow{M}}){\downarrow}} 
 (1)

 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = k + 1 n g ​ i ) ≃ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ( ∏ i = k + 1 n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={k+1}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{({\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={k+1}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{g\,n})}}} 
 (2) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m k g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ( ∏ i = k + 1 n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n ) ≃ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n \displaystyle\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{k}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{({\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={k+1}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{g\,n})}}\simeq{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{g\,n}}} 
 (3)

 (4) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;

(3) follows from Axiom 19 of  T T and the induction hypothesis.
Thus (b) holds by the Quasi-Equality Rules [ 20 , Lemma A.4] 
when n > k + 1 n>k+1 .
 Therefore, (b) holds for all n > k n>k , and (a) follows from this by the
Deduction Theorem [ 20 , Theorem A.50] and by Universal

 □ \Box 
 A.9 Development of COM-MON-over-COF 
 1. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟥𝟣 \mathsf{Thm31} :
 ∀ m , n : Z { R } , g , h : Z { R } → M . {\forall\,m,n:Z_{\{R\}},\,g,h:{Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{M}\mathrel{.}} 
 ( ∏ i = m n g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n h ​ i ) ≃ ∏ i = m n ( g ​ i ) ⋅ ( h ​ i ) {{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{h\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{{(g\,i)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(h\,i)}}}} 
 x (product of iterated products).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let 𝐀 o \mathbf{A}_{o} be the theorem and T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be
 COM-MON-over-COF extended by 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟫 \mathsf{Def9} . We must show ( a ) ​ T ⊨ 𝐀 o (a){\ }T\vDash\mathbf{A}_{o} .
 Let Δ \Delta be the set { n ∈ Z { R } , g ∈ Z { R } → M } \{{{n}\in{Z_{\{R\}}}},{{g}\in{{Z_{\{R\}}}\rightarrow{M}}}\} . We will prove
 ( b ) ​ Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ( ∏ i = m n g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n h ​ i ) ≃ ∏ i = m n ( g ​ i ) ⋅ ( h ​ i ) (\mbox{b}){\ }\Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{h\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{{(g\,i)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(h\,i)}}}} 
 for all n n by induction on the n n .
 Base case: n < m n<m . Then:
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( n : R ) ↓ ∧ ( g : R → M ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash{({n}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({g}:{{R}\rightarrow{M}}){\downarrow}} 
 (1) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m n g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n h ​ i ) ≃ 𝖾 ⋅ 𝖾 \displaystyle\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{h\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{{\mathsf{e}}\mathrel{\cdot}{\mathsf{e}}}} 
 (2) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ∏ i = m n ( g ​ i ) ⋅ ( h ​ i ) ≃ 𝖾 \displaystyle\vDash{{\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{{(g\,i)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(h\,i)}}}\simeq{\mathsf{e}}} 
 (3) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ; and
(2) and (3) follow from n < m n<m , (1), and 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟫 \mathsf{Def9} .
Thus (b) holds by Axiom 20 of T T and the Quasi-Equality Rules
 [ 20 , Lemma A.4] when n < m n<m .
 Induction step: n ≥ m n\geq m and assume 
 Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 h ​ i ) ≃ ∏ i = m n − 1 ( g ​ i ) ⋅ ( h ​ i ) . \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{h\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{{(g\,i)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(h\,i)}}}}. 
 Then:
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( n : R ) ↓ ∧ ( g : R → M ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash{({n}:{R}){\downarrow}}\land{({g}:{{R}\rightarrow{M}}){\downarrow}} 
 (1) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m n g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n h ​ i ) ≃ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 h ​ i ) ⋅ h ​ n \displaystyle\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{h\,i}\Big)}}\simeq{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{g\,n}}\mathrel{\cdot}{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{h\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{h\,n}}}} 
 (2) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 h ​ i ) ⋅ h ​ n ≃ \displaystyle\vDash{{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{g\,n}}\mathrel{\cdot}{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{h\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{h\,n}}}\simeq{}} 
 ( ∏ i = m n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 h ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n ⋅ h ​ n \displaystyle\phantom{{}\vDash{}}{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{h\,i}\Big)}}\mathrel{\cdot}{{g\,n}\mathrel{\cdot}{h\,n}}} 
 (3) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 g ​ i ) ⋅ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 h ​ i ) ⋅ g ​ n ⋅ h ​ n ≃ \displaystyle\vDash{{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{g\,i}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{h\,i}\Big)}}\mathrel{\cdot}{{g\,n}\mathrel{\cdot}{h\,n}}}\simeq{}} 
 ( ∏ i = m n − 1 ( g ​ i ) ⋅ ( h ​ i ) ) ⋅ ( g ​ n ⋅ h ​ n ) \displaystyle\phantom{{}\vDash{}}{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{{(g\,i)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(h\,i)}}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{({g\,n}\mathrel{\cdot}{h\,n})}} 
 (4) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( ∏ i = m n − 1 ( g ​ i ) ⋅ ( h ​ i ) ) ⋅ ( g ​ n ⋅ h ​ n ) ≃ ∏ i = m n ( g ​ i ) ⋅ ( h ​ i ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{\Big(\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n-1}{{(g\,i)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(h\,i)}}\Big)}\mathrel{\cdot}{({g\,n}\mathrel{\cdot}{h\,n})}}\simeq{\prod\limits_{{i}={m}}^{n}{{(g\,i)}\mathrel{\cdot}{(h\,i)}}}} 
 (5) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;
(2) and (5) follow from (1) and 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟫 \mathsf{Def9} ;
(3) follows from Axiom 21 of  T T ; and
(4) follows from the induction hypothesis.
Thus (b) holds by the Quasi-Equality Rules [ 20 , Lemma A.4] 
when n ≥ m n\geq m .
 Therefore, (b) holds for all n n , and (a) follows from this by the
Deduction Theorem [ 20 , Theorem A.50] and by Universal
Generalization [ 20 , Theorem A.30] using the fact that n n 
and g g are not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences.
 □ \Box 
 A.10 Development of COM-MON-ACT-over-COF 
 1. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟥𝟤 \mathsf{Thm32} :
 ∀ x , y : M , s : S . x 𝖺𝖼𝗍 ( y 𝖺𝖼𝗍 s ) = y 𝖺𝖼𝗍 ( x 𝖺𝖼𝗍 s ) {\forall\,x,y:M,\,s:S\mathrel{.}{{x}\mathrel{\mathsf{act}}{({y}\mathrel{\mathsf{act}}{s})}}={{y}\mathrel{\mathsf{act}}{({x}\mathrel{\mathsf{act}}{s})}}} 
 x ( 𝖺𝖼𝗍 \mathsf{act} has commutative-like property).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let 𝐀 o \mathbf{A}_{o} be the theorem and T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be
 COM-MON-ACT-over- COF . We must show ( ⋆ ) ​ T ⊨ 𝐀 o (\star){\ }T\vDash\mathbf{A}_{o} .
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ( x : M ) ↓ ∧ ( y : M ) ↓ ∧ ( s : S ) ↓ \displaystyle\vDash({x}:{M}){\downarrow}\land({y}:{M}){\downarrow}\land({s}:{S}){\downarrow} 
 (1) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ x ​ act ​ ( y ​ act ​ s ) = ( x ⋅ y ) ​ act ​ s \displaystyle\vDash x\>\textsf{act}\,(y\>\textsf{act}\,s)=(x\cdot y)\>\textsf{act}\,s 
 (2) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ y ​ act ​ ( x ​ act ​ s ) = ( y ⋅ x ) ​ act ​ s \displaystyle\vDash y\>\textsf{act}\,(x\>\textsf{act}\,s)=(y\cdot x)\>\textsf{act}\,s 
 (3) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ x ⋅ y = y ⋅ x \displaystyle\vDash{x}\mathrel{\cdot}{y}={y}\mathrel{\cdot}{x} 
 (4) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ y ​ act ​ ( x ​ act ​ s ) = ( x ⋅ y ) ​ act ​ s \displaystyle\vDash y\>\textsf{act}\,(x\>\textsf{act}\,s)=(x\cdot y)\>\textsf{act}\,s 
 (5) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ x ​ act ​ ( y ​ act ​ s ) = y ​ act ​ ( x ​ act ​ s ) \displaystyle\vDash x\>\textsf{act}\,(y\>\textsf{act}\,s)=y\>\textsf{act}\,(x\>\textsf{act}\,s) 
 (6) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ 𝐀 o \displaystyle\vDash{\mathbf{A}_{o}} 
 (7) 
 (1) follows from variables always being defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.1] ;
(2) and (3) follow from (1) and Axiom 22 of T T by
Universal Instantiation [ 20 , Theorem A.14] ;
(4) follows from (1) and Axiom 21 of T T by Universal Instantiation;
(5) follows from (4) and (3) by Quasi-Equality Substitution [ 20 , Lemma A.2] ;
(6) follows from (2) and (5) by the Equality Rules  [ 20 , Lemma A.13] ;
(7) follows from (6) by Universal Generalization [ 20 , Theorem A.30] 
using the fact that x x , y y , and s s are not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a set of
sentences. Therefore, ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds. 
 □ \Box 
 A.11 Development of STR 
 1. 
 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟣𝟢 \mathsf{Def10} :
 𝗌𝗍𝗋 { R → A } = [ A ] {{\mathsf{str}_{\{{R}\rightarrow{A}\}}}={[A]}} 
 (string quasitype).
 Proof that RHS is defined. 
Let T T be the top theory of STR-1 . We must show ( ⋆ ) (\star) 
 T ⊨ [ A ] ↓ T\vDash[A]{\downarrow} . Now [ A ] [A] stands for
 { s : ⟨ ⟨ A ⟩ ⟩ ∣ ∃ n : C { R } N . ∀ m : C { R } N . ( s m ) ↓ ⇔ C A → A → o m n } \{{{s}:{\langle\!\langle A\rangle\!\rangle}}\mid{\exists\,n:\textbf{C}_{\{R\}}^{N}\mathrel{.}\forall\,m:\textbf{C}_{\{R\}}^{N}\mathrel{.}(s\>m){\downarrow}\Leftrightarrow\textbf{C}_{{A}\rightarrow{{A}\rightarrow{o}}}m\>n}\} 
 based on
the notational definitions in Table 10.1 in [ 20 ] . Thus
 ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds because function abstractions are always defined
by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.11] .
 □ \Box 
 2. 
 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟣𝟣 \mathsf{Def11} :
 ϵ R → A = [ ] R → A {{\epsilon_{{R}\rightarrow{A}}}={{[\;]}_{{R}\rightarrow{A}}}} (empty string).
 Proof that RHS is defined. 
Let T T be the top theory of STR-1 . We must show ( ⋆ ) (\star) 
 T ⊨ [ ] R → A ↓ T\vDash{{[\;]}_{{R}\rightarrow{A}}}{\downarrow} . Now [ ] R → A {[\;]}_{{R}\rightarrow{A}} 
stands for
 λ x : R . ⊥ A \lambda\,x:R\mathrel{.}\bot_{A} 
 based on the notational definitions in Tables 6.4 and 10.1 in
 [ 20 ] . Thus ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds because function abstractions
are always defined by  [ 20 , Axiom A5.11] .
 □ \Box 
 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟣𝟤 \mathsf{Def12} :
 𝖼𝖺𝗍 ( ( R → A ) × ( R → A ) ) → ( R → A ) = {{\mathsf{cat}_{{({({R}\rightarrow{A})}\times{({R}\rightarrow{A})})}\rightarrow{({R}\rightarrow{A})}}}={}} 
 I ​ f : ( 𝗌𝗍𝗋 × 𝗌𝗍𝗋 ) → 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . {\mathrm{I}\,f:{({\mathsf{str}}\times{\mathsf{str}})}\rightarrow{\mathsf{str}}\mathrel{.}} 
 ∀ x : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . f ⁡ ( ϵ , x ) = x ∧ {{\forall\,x:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{f\,(\epsilon,x)}={x}}\wedge{}} 
 ∀ a : A , x , y : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . f ( a : : x , y ) = a : : f ( x , y ) {\forall\,a:A,\,x,y:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{f\,({a}::{x},y)}={{a}::{f\,(x,y)}}} .
 (concatenation).
 Proof that RHS is defined. 
Let T T be the top theory of STR-1 extended by Def10 
and Def11 . We must show
 ( ⋆ ) ​ T ⊨ 𝖼𝖺𝗍 ( ( R → A ) × ( R → A ) ) → ( R → A ) ↓ . (\star){\ }T\vDash{\mathsf{cat}_{{({({R}\rightarrow{A})}\times{({R}\rightarrow{A})})}\rightarrow{({R}\rightarrow{A})}}{\downarrow}}. 
 The body of the RHS of the definition
uniquely specifies the concatenation function of members of
 𝗌𝗍𝗋 \mathsf{str} . Thus ( ⋆ ) (\star) holds by  [ 20 , Axioms A5.8 and
A6.1] .
 □ \Box 
 3. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟥𝟥 \mathsf{Thm33} :
 ∀ x : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . ϵ ​ x = x ​ ϵ = x {\forall\,x:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{{\epsilon}{x}}\mathrel{=}{{x}{\epsilon}}\mathrel{=}{x}} 
 ( ϵ \epsilon is an identity element).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be the top theory of STR-1 extended
by Def10–Def12 . We must show:
 (a)
 T ⊨ ∀ x : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . ϵ ​ x = x . T\vDash{\forall\,x:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{{\epsilon}{x}}={x}}. 
 (b)
 T ⊨ ∀ x : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . x ​ ϵ = x . T\vDash{\forall\,x:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{{x}{\epsilon}}={x}}. 
 Let Δ \Delta be the set { x ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 } \{{x}\in{\mathsf{str}}\} . Then:
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ϵ ​ x = x \displaystyle\vDash{{{\epsilon}{x}}={x}} 
 (1) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊨ ∀ x : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . ϵ ​ x = x \displaystyle\vDash{\forall\,x:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{{\epsilon}{x}}={x}} 
 (2) 
 (1) follows from x ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 {x}\in{\mathsf{str}} and 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟣𝟤 \mathsf{Def12} ; and
(2) follows from (1) by the Deduction Theorem [ 20 , Theorem
A.50] and then by Universal Generalization [ 20 , Theorem
A.30] using the fact that ( x : R → A ) ({x}:{{R}\rightarrow{A}}) is
not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a set of sentences.
Therefore, (a) holds.
 We will prove (c) Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ x ​ ϵ = x \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{x}{\epsilon}}={x}} by induction on the length of x x .
 Base case: x x is ϵ \epsilon . Then Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ϵ ​ ϵ = ϵ \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{\epsilon}{\epsilon}}={\epsilon}} is an
instance of (1) above.
 Induction step: x x is ( a : : y ) ({a}::{y}) and assume Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ y ​ ϵ = y \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{y}{\epsilon}}={y}} . Then:
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( a : : y ) ϵ = ( a : : y ϵ ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{({a}::{y})}{\epsilon}}={({a}::{{y}{\epsilon}})}} 
 (1) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( a : : y ) ϵ = ( a : : y ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{({a}::{y})}{\epsilon}}={({a}::{y})}} 
 (2) 
 (1) follows from x ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 {x}\in{\mathsf{str}} and 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟣𝟤 \mathsf{Def12} ; and
(2) follows from the induction hypothesis and (1) by Quasi-Equality
Substitution [ 20 , Lemma A.2] . Thus Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ( a : : y ) ϵ = ( a : : y ) \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{({a}::{y})}{\epsilon}}={({a}::{y})}} holds by the Equality Rules  [ 20 , Lemma A.13] .
 Therefore (c) holds, and (b) follows from (c) by the Deduction Theorem
 [ 20 , Theorem A.50] and then by Universal Generalization
 [ 20 , Theorem A.30] using the fact that
 ( x : R → A ) ({x}:{{R}\rightarrow{A}}) is not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is a
set of sentences.
 □ \Box 
 4. 
 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟥𝟦 \mathsf{Thm34} :
 ∀ x , y , z : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . x ⁡ ( y ​ z ) = ( x ​ y ) ​ z {\forall\,x,y,z:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{{x}{({y}{z})}}={{({x}{y})}{z}}} 
 ( 𝖼𝖺𝗍 \mathsf{cat} is associative).
 Proof of the theorem. 
Let T = ( L , Γ ) T=(L,\Gamma) be the top theory of STR-1 extended
by Def10–Def12 . We must show
 (a) ​ T ⊨ ∀ x , y , z : 𝗌𝗍𝗋 . x ⁡ ( y ​ z ) = ( x ​ y ) ​ z . \mbox{(a)}{\ }T\vDash{\forall\,x,y,z:\mathsf{str}\mathrel{.}{{x}{({y}{z})}}={{({x}{y})}{z}}}. 
 Let Δ \Delta be the set { x ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 , y ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 , z ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 } \{{x}\in{\mathsf{str}},{y}\in{\mathsf{str}},{z}\in{\mathsf{str}}\} . We will prove
 (b) ​ Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ x ⁡ ( y ​ z ) = ( x ​ y ) ​ z \mbox{(b)}{\ }\Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{x}{({y}{z})}}={{({x}{y})}{z}}} 
 by induction on the length of x x .
 Base case: x x is ϵ \epsilon . Then:
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ϵ ⁡ ( y ​ z ) = ( y ​ z ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{\epsilon}{({y}{z})}}={({y}{z})}} 
 (1) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( y ​ z ) = ( ϵ ​ y ) ​ z \displaystyle\vDash{{({y}{z})}={{({\epsilon}{y})}{z}}} 
 (2) 
 (1) and (2) follow from 𝖳𝗁𝗆𝟥𝟥 \mathsf{Thm33} . Thus Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ϵ ⁡ ( y ​ z ) = ( ϵ ​ y ) ​ z \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{\epsilon}{({y}{z})}}={{({\epsilon}{y})}{z}}} holds by the Equality
Rules  [ 20 , Lemma A.13] .
 Induction step: x x is ( a : : w ) ({a}::{w}) and assume Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ w ⁡ ( y ​ z ) = ( w ​ y ) ​ z \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{w}{({y}{z})}}={{({w}{y})}{z}}} . Then:
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( a : : w ) ( y z ) = a : : w ( y z ) \displaystyle\vDash{{{({a}::{w})}{({y}{z})}}={{a}::{{w}{({y}{z})}}}} 
 (1) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ a : : w ⁡ ( y ​ z ) = a : : ( w ​ y ) ​ z \displaystyle\vDash{{{a}::{{w}{({y}{z})}}}={{a}::{{({w}{y})}{z}}}} 
 (2) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ a : : ( w y ) z = ( a : : w y ) z \displaystyle\vDash{{{a}::{{({w}{y})}{z}}}={{({a}::{{w}{y}})}{z}}} 
 (3) 
 Γ ∪ Δ \displaystyle\Gamma\cup\Delta 
 ⊨ ( a : : w y ) z = ( ( a : : w ) y ) z \displaystyle\vDash{{{({a}::{{w}{y}})}{z}}={{({({a}::{w})}{y})}{z}}} 
 (4) 
 (1), (3), and (4) follow from x ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 {x}\in{\mathsf{str}} , y ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 {y}\in{\mathsf{str}} , and z ∈ 𝗌𝗍𝗋 {z}\in{\mathsf{str}} and
 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟣𝟤 \mathsf{Def12} ; and (2) follows from the induction hypothesis.
Thus
 Γ ∪ Δ ⊨ ( a : : w ) ( y z ) = ( ( a : : w ) y ) z \Gamma\cup\Delta\vDash{{{({a}::{w})}{({y}{z})}}={{({({a}::{w})}{y})}{z}}} 
 holds by the Equality Rules  [ 20 , Lemma A.13] .
 Therefore (b) holds, and (a) follows from (b) by the Deduction Theorem
 [ 20 , Theorem A.50] and then by Universal Generalization
 [ 20 , Theorem A.30] using the fact that
 ( x : R → A ) ({x}:{{R}\rightarrow{A}}) , ( y : R → A ) ({y}:{{R}\rightarrow{A}}) , and
 ( z : R → A ) ({z}:{{R}\rightarrow{A}}) are not free in Γ \Gamma since Γ \Gamma is
a set of sentences.
 □ \Box

 Appendix B Miscellaneous Theorems
 Lemma B.1 (Universal Sets) 
 The following formulas are valid: 
 1. 
 U { α } ↓ {U_{\{\alpha\}}{\downarrow}} . 
 2. 
 U { α } ≠ ∅ { α } {{U_{\{\alpha\}}}\not={\emptyset_{\{\alpha\}}}} . 
 3. 
 ∀ x : α . x ∈ U { α } {\forall\,x:\alpha\mathrel{.}{x}\in{U_{\{\alpha\}}}} . 
 4. 
 ( λ 𝐱 : α . 𝐁 β ) = ( λ 𝐱 : U { α } . 𝐁 β ) {{(\lambda\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{\beta})}={(\lambda\,\mathbf{x}:U_{\{\alpha\}}\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{\beta})}} . 
 5. 
 ( ∀ 𝐱 : α . 𝐁 o ) ⇔ ( ∀ 𝐱 : U { α } . 𝐁 o ) {{(\forall\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{o})}\Leftrightarrow{(\forall\,\mathbf{x}:U_{\{\alpha\}}\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{o})}} . 
 6. 
 ( ∃ 𝐱 : α . 𝐁 o ) ⇔ ( ∃ 𝐱 : U { α } . 𝐁 o ) {{(\exists\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{o})}\Leftrightarrow{(\exists\,\mathbf{x}:U_{\{\alpha\}}\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{o})}} . 
 7. 
 ( I 𝐱 : α . 𝐁 o ) = ( I 𝐱 : U { α } . 𝐁 o ) {{(\mathrm{I}\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{o})}={(\mathrm{I}\,\mathbf{x}:U_{\{\alpha\}}\mathrel{.}\mathbf{B}_{o})}} . 
 8. 
 𝐀 α ↓ ⇔ ( 𝐀 α ↓ U { α } ) {{\mathbf{A}_{\alpha}{\downarrow}}\Leftrightarrow{({\mathbf{A}_{\alpha}}\downarrow{U_{\{\alpha\}}})}} 
 9. 
 U { α → β } = ( U { α } → U { β } ) {{U_{\{{\alpha}\rightarrow{\beta}\}}}={({U_{\{\alpha\}}}\rightarrow{U_{\{\beta\}}})}} . 
 10. 
 U { α × β } = ( U { α } × U { β } ) {{U_{\{{\alpha}\times{\beta}\}}}={({U_{\{\alpha\}}}\times{U_{\{\beta\}}})}} . 
 11. 
 U { { α } } = 𝒫 ⁡ ( U { α } ) {{U_{\{\{\alpha\}\}}}={{\cal P}(U_{\{\alpha\}})}} . 
 12. 
 A ( α × β ) → γ = A ( α × β ) → γ ↾ U { α } × U { β } {{\textbf{A}_{{({\alpha}\times{\beta})}\rightarrow{\gamma}}}={\textbf{A}_{{({\alpha}\times{\beta})}\rightarrow{\gamma}}\mbox{$\upharpoonright$}_{{U_{\{\alpha\}}}\times{U_{\{\beta\}}}}}} . 
 Proof The proof is left to the reader as an exercise.
 □ \Box 
 The following lemma is an expanded version of [ 20 , Lemma
13.9] :
 Lemma B.2 
 Let Φ = ( μ , ν ) \Phi=(\mu,\nu) be a translation from T 1 T_{1} to T 2 T_{2} . 
 1. 
 If α ∈ 𝒯 ⁡ ( L 1 ) \alpha\in{\cal T}(L_{1}) and μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ∈ 𝒬 2 \overline{\mu}(\alpha)\in{\cal Q}_{2} , then T 2 ⊨ μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ↓ ⇒ ν ¯ ​ ( U { α } ) = μ ¯ ​ ( α ) T_{2}\vDash{\overline{\mu}(\alpha){\downarrow}}\Rightarrow{{\overline{\nu}(U_{\{\alpha\}})}={\overline{\mu}(\alpha)}} . 
 2. 
 If α ∈ 𝒯 ⁡ ( L 1 ) \alpha\in{\cal T}(L_{1}) and μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ∈ 𝒬 2 \overline{\mu}(\alpha)\in{\cal Q}_{2} , then T 2 ⊨ μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ↑ ⇒ ν ¯ ​ ( U { α } ) = ∅ { τ ​ ( μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ) } T_{2}\vDash{\overline{\mu}(\alpha){\uparrow}}\Rightarrow{{\overline{\nu}(U_{\{\alpha\}})}={\emptyset_{\{\tau(\overline{\mu}(\alpha))\}}}} . 
 3. 
 If 𝐚 ∈ ℬ 1 \mathbf{a}\in{\cal B}_{1} and μ ⁡ ( 𝐚 ) ∈ 𝒬 2 \mu(\mathbf{a})\in{\cal Q}_{2} ,
then 
 T 2 ⊨ ν ¯ ​ ( U { 𝐚 } ≠ ∅ { 𝐚 } ) ⇔ ( μ ⁡ ( 𝐚 ) ↓ ∧ μ ⁡ ( 𝐚 ) ≠ ∅ { τ ⁡ ( μ ⁡ ( 𝐚 ) ) } ) . T_{2}\vDash{\overline{\nu}({U_{\{\mathbf{a}\}}}\not={\emptyset_{\{\mathbf{a}\}}})}\Leftrightarrow{({\mu(\mathbf{a}){\downarrow}}\wedge{{\mu(\mathbf{a})}\not={\emptyset_{\{\tau(\mu(\mathbf{a}))\}}}})}. 
 4. 
 If 𝐜 α ∈ 𝒞 1 {{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha}}\in{\cal C}_{1} and
 μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ∈ 𝒯 2 \overline{\mu}(\alpha)\in{\cal T}_{2} , then T 2 ⊨ ν ¯ ​ ( 𝐜 α ↓ U { α } ) ⇔ ν ⁡ ( 𝐜 α ) ↓ T_{2}\vDash{\overline{\nu}({{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha}}\downarrow{U_{\{\alpha\}}})}\Leftrightarrow{\nu({\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha}){\downarrow}} . 
 5. 
 If 𝐜 α ∈ 𝒞 1 {{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha}}\in{\cal C}_{1} and
 μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ∈ 𝒬 2 \overline{\mu}(\alpha)\in{\cal Q}_{2} , then 
 T 2 ⊨ μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ↓ ⇒ ν ¯ ​ ( 𝐜 α ↓ U { α } ) ⇔ ν ⁡ ( 𝐜 α ) ↓ μ ¯ ​ ( α ) . T_{2}\vDash{\overline{\mu}(\alpha){\downarrow}}\Rightarrow{{\overline{\nu}({{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha}}\downarrow{U_{\{\alpha\}}})}\Leftrightarrow{{\nu({\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha})}\downarrow{\overline{\mu}(\alpha)}}}. 
 Proof Let α ∈ 𝒯 ⁡ ( L 1 ) \alpha\in{\cal T}(L_{1}) , μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ∈ 𝒬 2 \overline{\mu}(\alpha)\in{\cal Q}_{2} , and
 ( ⋆ ) (\star) M M be a general model of T 2 T_{2} in which μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ↓ {\overline{\mu}(\alpha){\downarrow}} is true. We must show that ν ¯ ​ ( U { α } ) = μ ¯ ​ ( α ) {{\overline{\nu}(U_{\{\alpha\}})}={\overline{\mu}(\alpha)}} is
true in M M . Then
 ν ¯ ​ ( U { α } ) \displaystyle\phantom{{}\equiv{}}\overline{\nu}(U_{\{\alpha\}}) 
 ≡ ν ¯ ( λ x : α . T o ) \displaystyle\equiv\overline{\nu}(\lambda\,x:\alpha\mathrel{.}T_{o}) 
 ≡ λ ​ x : μ ¯ ​ ( α ) . T o \displaystyle\equiv\lambda\,x:\overline{\mu}(\alpha)\mathrel{.}T_{o} 
 ≡ λ ​ x : τ ⁡ ( μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ) . ( x ∈ μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ↦ T o ∣ F o ) . \displaystyle\equiv\lambda\,x:\tau(\overline{\mu}(\alpha))\mathrel{.}({x}\in{\overline{\mu}(\alpha)}\mapsto T_{o}\mid F_{o}). 
 by the definition of ν ¯ \overline{\nu} and notational definitions. The
last expression is clearly equal to λ ​ x : τ ⁡ ( μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ) . μ ¯ ​ ( α ) ​ x \lambda\,x:\tau(\overline{\mu}(\alpha))\mathrel{.}\overline{\mu}(\alpha)\,x , which is equal to μ ¯ ​ ( α ) \overline{\mu}(\alpha) in M M 
by  ( ⋆ ) (\star) . This proves part 1. Part 2 follows from the proof of
part 1 and the notational definition for the empty set pseudoconstant.
Part 3 follows immediately from the definition of ν ¯ \overline{\nu} and
parts 1 and 2. Part 4 follows from the definition of ν ¯ \overline{\nu} 
and the notational definition for the defined-in-quasitype operator.

 □ \Box 
 Theorem B.3 (Existential Instantiation) 
 If Γ ⊢ 𝔄 𝐀 o ⇒ 𝐁 o \Gamma\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{\mathbf{A}_{o}}\Rightarrow{\mathbf{B}_{o}}} , then 
 Γ ⊢ 𝔄 ( ∃ 𝐱 : α . 𝐀 o ) ⇒ 𝐁 o , \Gamma\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{(\exists\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\mathbf{A}_{o})}\Rightarrow{\mathbf{B}_{o}}}, 
 provided that ( 𝐱 : α ) {({\mathbf{x}}:{\alpha})} 
is not free in 𝐁 o \mathbf{B}_{o} or in any member of Γ \Gamma . 
 Proof Let ( ⋆ ) (\star) ( 𝐱 : α ) ({\mathbf{x}}:{\alpha}) be not free in
 𝐁 o \mathbf{B}_{o} or in any member of Γ \Gamma .
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊢ 𝔄 𝐀 o ⇒ 𝐁 o \displaystyle\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{\mathbf{A}_{o}}\Rightarrow{\mathbf{B}_{o}}} 
 (1) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊢ ¬ 𝔄 ⁡ 𝐁 o ⇒ ¬ 𝐀 o \displaystyle\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{\neg{\mathbf{B}_{o}}}\Rightarrow{\neg{\mathbf{A}_{o}}}} 
 (2) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊢ ¬ 𝔄 ⁡ 𝐁 o ⇒ ∀ 𝐱 : α . ¬ 𝐀 o \displaystyle\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{\neg{\mathbf{B}_{o}}}\Rightarrow{\forall\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\neg{\mathbf{A}_{o}}}} 
 (3) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊢ ¬ 𝔄 ⁡ 𝐁 o ⇒ ¬ ¬ ∀ ⁡ 𝐱 : α . ¬ 𝐀 o \displaystyle\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{\neg{\mathbf{B}_{o}}}\Rightarrow{\neg{\neg{\forall\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\neg{\mathbf{A}_{o}}}}}} 
 (4) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊢ ¬ 𝔄 ⁡ 𝐁 o ⇒ ¬ ∃ ⁡ 𝐱 : α . 𝐀 o \displaystyle\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{\neg{\mathbf{B}_{o}}}\Rightarrow{\neg{\exists\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\mathbf{A}_{o}}}} 
 (5) 
 Γ \displaystyle\Gamma 
 ⊢ 𝔄 ( ∃ 𝐱 : α . 𝐀 o ) ⇒ 𝐁 o \displaystyle\vdash_{\mathfrak{A}}{{(\exists\,\mathbf{x}:\alpha\mathrel{.}\mathbf{A}_{o})}\Rightarrow{\mathbf{B}_{o}}} 
 (6) 
 (1) is given; (2) follows from (1), (4) follows from (3), and (6)
follows from (5) by the Tautology Rule [ 20 , Corollary
A.46] ; (3) follows from (2) and ( ⋆ ) (\star) by [ 20 , Lemma
A.47] and (5) follows from (4) by the notational
definition for ∃ \exists .
 □ \Box 
 Lemma B.4 
 Let T T be 𝖬𝖮𝖭 \mathsf{MON} extended by the definition 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟤 \mathsf{Def2} .
The formula 
 A M ⋅ op B M ≃ B M ⋅ A M {{{\textbf{A}_{M}}\mathrel{\cdot^{\rm op}}{\textbf{B}_{M}}}\simeq{{\textbf{B}_{M}}\mathrel{\cdot}{\textbf{A}_{M}}}} 
 is valid in T T . 
 Proof Let X o \textbf{X}_{o} be
 A M ⋅ op B M ≃ B M ⋅ A M , {{{\textbf{A}_{M}}\mathrel{\cdot^{\rm op}}{\textbf{B}_{M}}}\simeq{{\textbf{B}_{M}}\mathrel{\cdot}{\textbf{A}_{M}}}}, 
 N N be a model of T T , and φ ∈ 𝖺𝗌𝗌𝗂𝗀𝗇 ⁡ ( N ) \varphi\in\mathsf{assign}(N) . Suppose that
 V φ N ​ ( A M ) V^{N}_{\varphi}(\textbf{A}_{M}) or V φ N ​ ( B M ) V^{N}_{\varphi}(\textbf{B}_{M}) is
undefined. Then clearly V φ N ​ ( X o ) = t V^{N}_{\varphi}(\textbf{X}_{o})=\mbox{{\sc t}} . Now
suppose that V φ N ​ ( A M ) V^{N}_{\varphi}(\textbf{A}_{M}) and
 V φ N ​ ( B M ) V^{N}_{\varphi}(\textbf{B}_{M}) are defined. Then
 V φ N ​ ( X o ) = t V^{N}_{\varphi}(\textbf{X}_{o})=\mbox{{\sc t}} by 𝖣𝖾𝖿𝟤 \mathsf{Def2} .
 □ \Box
